2026 年 10 月 2 日 全8 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 8 月雇用統計 
失業率は2.5％と前月から0.1％pt 上昇も、雇用環境は概ね横ばい

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年 8 月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.5％と、5 カ月ぶりに上昇（前月差＋
0.1％pt）し、4～6 月の水準に戻った。失業者数は 2 カ月ぶりに増加（同＋10 万人）
したものの自発的な離職が中心で、就業者数は小幅ながら3 カ月ぶりに増加（同＋3 万
人）した。総じて見れば、雇用環境は前月から概ね横ばいで推移したとみられる。 
◼ 2026 年8 月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.18 倍と前月から横ばいだった。他方、
新規求人倍率は2.06 倍（前月差▲0.04pt）と前月から低下した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、 雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢を めぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

2 / 8

8 月の完全失業率：2.5％と前月から上昇も、失業者の増加は「自発的な離職」が中心 
2026 年 8 月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.5％と、5 カ月ぶりに上昇（前月差＋0.1％pt）
し、4～6 月の水準に戻った（図表 2 左上） 。失業者数は2 カ月ぶりに増加（同＋10 万人）し、
就業者数は小幅ながら 3 カ月ぶりに増加（同＋3 万人）した。総じて見れば、雇用環境は前月
から概ね横ばいで推移したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差＋6 万人）が増加し、全体を押し
上げた。ほかにも「定年又は雇用契約の満了」は同＋2 万人、「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」
は同＋1 万人、「新たに求職」は同＋1 万人と、幅広く増加した（巻末の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化 を業種別に見ると、「学術研究，専門・技術サービス業 」など一
部の非製造業の増加が目立った。5、6 月に下振れしていた反動が表れたとみられる。「卸売業，
小売業」も増加した。他方、「製造業」、「建設業」、 「情報通信業」は減少した。「医療，福祉」
は概ね横ばいだった（図表 2 左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から横ばいだった一方、
非正規雇用者は4 カ月連続で減少（前月差▲10 万人）した（図表2 右下）。 
図表 2：就業者数・完全失業率（左上） 、非労働力人口・労働参加率（右上） 、 
業種別就業者数（左下） 、雇用形態別雇用者数（右下）

3 / 8

8 月の新規求人倍率：求職が増加した一方で求人は減少し、前月から低下 
2026 年 8 月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.18 倍と前月から横ばいだった。他方、新規
求人倍率は 2.06 倍（前月差▲0.04pt）と前月から低下した（図表3 左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数（前月比＋0.7％）は2 カ月ぶりに増加した一方、新規求
人数（同▲1.5％）は 3 カ月ぶりに減少した（図表 4 左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同＋
0.7％）、新規求職申込件数（同＋0.6％）はいずれも 2 カ月連続で増加した。求職側は 2025 年
春頃から持ち直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026 年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.00 倍と3 カ月連続で同水準
だった。新規求人倍率は1.68 倍（前月差▲0.07pt）と2 カ月連続で低下した（図表3 右）。 
図表 3：有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率（左：全数、右：正社員）

（注）季節調整値。

4 / 8

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026 年 9 月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％pt と 6 月調査から 1％pt 低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％pt と更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％pt と6 月調査から3％pt 低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％pt だった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。特に原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化さ
せ3、幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7 回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300 人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3 年連続の 5％超え！～2026 春季生活闘争 第7 回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026 年7 月3 日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122 条に基づく措置が失効する 7 月 24 日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026 年7 月 24 日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026 年
8 月」（大和総研レポート、2026 年8 月 24 日）を参照。

Document 163: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

Document 174: AI大神Karpathy最新洞察：未来我们更该“理解”大模型输出，而不是被它淹没

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI大神Karpathy最新洞察：未来我们更该“理解”大模型输出，而不是被它淹没.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼤模型越来越强，⽣成内容越来越⻓、越来越复杂，⼈类反⽽容易陷⼊ “ 信息过载 ” 。  真正
的价值，不再是 “ 让它⽣成 ” ，⽽是 “ 如何⾼效、清晰地理解它⽣成的东西 ” 。
摘要：
最近， AI 领域的传奇⼈物 Andrej Karpathy （前 OpenAI 联合创始⼈、特斯拉 AI 总监）在 X 上发了
⼀条极具前瞻性的帖⼦，迅速引发⼴泛讨论。
他开⻔⻅⼭地说： “ 我们将花越来越多的时间去理解语⾔模型的输出。 ”
这句话看似简单，却精准点出了当前 AI 使⽤的核⼼痛点：⼤模型越来越强，⽣成内容越来越⻓、
越来越复杂，⼈类反⽽容易陷⼊ “ 信息过载 ” 。  真正的价值，不再是 “ 让它⽣成 ” ，⽽是 “ 如何⾼
效、清晰地理解它⽣成的东西 ” 。
Karpathy 分享了⼏个他亲⾃验证过的实⽤技巧，从⽂字到图表，再到交互⽹⻚和解释视频，层
层递进。今天我们就来了解⼀下这些⽅法，并补充相关背景知识，希望帮助⼤家在实际⼯作中
真正落地。
写作层⾯：⽤ ASD-STE100“ 控制语⾔ ” 逼出清晰表达

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Karpathy 最推荐的⽅法之⼀是：直接要求⼤模型⽤ ASD-STE100 来解释内容。
ASD-STE100 全称是 Simplified Technical English （简化技术英语），最初是欧洲航空航天⼯业
在上世纪 80 年代为⻜机维修⼿册开发的受控⾃然语⾔。⽬的很明确：就是让⾮英语⺟语的技术
⼈员也能准确弄懂关键安全信息，避免因歧义导致事故。
它的核⼼特点包括：
• 约 900 个批准词汇，每个词只有⼀个明确含义
• 严格的写作规则：主动语态、短句、⼀句话⼀个指令、避免被动语态和不必要的同义词
• 句⼦⻓度和段落结构都有明确限制
Karpathy 发现，⼤模型对这种规范⾮常熟悉，⽣成的⽂字往往简洁、⼲净、易读。如果觉得太
严格，可以稍微放松，⽐如要求 “ 达到 ASD-STE100 的 80% 程度 ” 。
实际体验下来，⽤这种⻛格解释复杂技术概念时，冗余⼤幅减少，逻辑更清晰，特别适合技术
⽂档、产品说明或内部知识沉淀。
更进⼀步：直接⽣成图表和图像
⽂字再清晰，有时也不如图表直观。

Karpathy 建议：别只让它写，直接让⼤模型⽣成图表或⽰意图。视觉信息往往⽐纯⽂字更容易
处理和记忆。⽆论是流程图、架构图还是概念对⽐图，⼤模型现在的视觉⽣成能⼒已经相当成
熟，能把抽象概念具象化。
再升级：输出成精美可交互的 HTML ⽹⻚
⽐图表更强的是完整⽹⻚。
只需在提⽰词⾥加上 “ ⽤ HTML 输出 ” ，⼤模型就能⽣成带样式、动画甚⾄简单交互的⻚⾯。前端
能⼒近年突⻜猛进，⽣成的⻚⾯往往美观且实⽤，适合⽤来做演⽰、知识库或临时⼯具。
这种⽅式直接把 “ 阅读 ” 变成了 “ 体验 ” ，理解成本进⼀步降低。
最看好的⽅向：定制化解释视频（ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格）
Karpathy 最看好的输出形式，是完全定制的解释视频。
他举例：可以要求 “ ⽤ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格制作关于 X 主题的解释视频，并⽤ ElevenLabs API 做语
⾳旁⽩ ” 。
3Blue1Brown （ Grant Sanderson ）以精美的数学动画闻名，其开源动画库 Manim 已被社区⼴泛

使⽤。现在⼤模型已经能较好地⽣成 Manim 代码，再结合语⾳合成，确实能够产出可⽤的教学
视频。
这可能代表⼀种范式转变：过去为理解⼀个复杂概念，我们需要花费⼤量时间阅读或等待专业
视频出现；现在已经可以直接按需⽣成 “ 专属解释视频 ” 。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
158 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
从 “ 使⽤ AI” 到 “ 监督与理解 AI”
Karpathy 在帖⼦结尾点出了更深⼀层的趋势：随着⼤模型能⼒持续提升，它们会越来越多地⾃
主完成底层⼯作。⼈类的⼯作会向更⾼抽象层级移动，那就是监督、理解和把关。
幸运的是，⼤模型本⾝也能帮我们完成这⼀转变。因为智能和代码变得极其丰富，我们可以让
它⽣成那些过去 “ 不值得专⻔制作 ” 的⼤型、定制、可抛弃的软件产物（⽹⻚、视频、交互⼯具
等）。
⼤胆尝试这些更⾼维度的输出形式，往往会带来惊喜。
具体实践建议（可以直接复制使⽤）：
• “ ⽤ ASD-STE100 ⻛格解释 [ 主题 ] ，要求清晰、主动语态、短句。 ”
• “ 为 [ 主题 ] ⽣成⼀张清晰的架构图 / 流程图。 ”
• “ ⽤ HTML 创建⼀个美观的交互式⽹⻚，解释 [ 主题 ] 。 ”

• “ ⽤ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格制作关于 [ 主题 ] 的解释视频脚本，并给出 Manim 代码思路。 ”
AI 真正改变⼯作⽅式的关键，不在于它能⽣成多少内容，⽽在于我们能否⾼效理解它⽣成的内
容。 Karpathy 的这条帖⼦，给所有 AI 重度⽤⼾指明了⼀条清晰的路径。

Document 195: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

TUẦN 21-25/09 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tu ần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Cập nhật công ty] 
FPT , REE, POW, AST , BWE

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn 
chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX đư ợc kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ gần 
quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

### Page 5

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh đư ợc 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi 
bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn 
(Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

Document 219: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

At current levels, the US 10-year government bond yield 
offers investors compelling risk-reward trade off 
Implied 1-year FX-hedged total returns (%) on various 10-
year Developed Market government bonds from shifts in 
their yields; grey dotted lines roughly denote current spot 
yield levels

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations 
Our policy rate outlook for the Fed, ECB and BoJ

tariffs, long-term inflation expectations remain subdued

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

tariff effects on inflation to fade over 12 months, 
offsetting AI-driven effects on bond yields

Document 220: Cooler US jobs growth supports a Federal Reserve pause in October

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cooler US jobs growth supports a Federal Reserve pause in October.pdf

### Page 1

In the wake of dovish-leaning comments from the Fed
leadership and softer-than-anticipated inﬂation, today's
weaker-than-predicted jobs ﬁgures have further diminished the
chances of an October interest rate hike. December remains
the most likely date for the next move. We continue to believe
market pricing for additional action is too aggressive
James Knightley

one of the bright spots in a weaker-than-expected nonfarm payrolls number

### Page 2

The US September jobs report is softer than expected on all three
key metrics. 1) non-farm payrolls rose +29k versus the +90k
consensus with 60k of downward revisions, 2) the unemployment
rate ticks up to 4.2% from 4.1%, driven by a partial recovery in the
participation rate (people returning to the workforce) and 3) and
wage growth of 3% year-on-year versus 3.1%, suggesting that
there is no inﬂation threat emanating from the jobs market.
It chimes with the latest comments from Federal Reserve big hitters

John Williams (NY Fed president) and Philip Jeﬀerson (Vice Chair),
suggesting they may need more time/no urgency to hike rates. As
such, the Fed leadership is clearly leaning in the direction of an
October hold, meaning it will require a hot September CPI print (due
14 October) to make the meeting interesting. December remains
our call on the next Fed hike.
In terms of some of the details, manufacturing is the bright spot
with the fourth consecutive monthly rise and 72,000 jobs

cumulatively added since the start of the year. This is consistent
with strong survey readings for the sector, with advanced
manufacturing (pharma, transport & aerospace, computing &
electronics) performing particularly well. Construction also
increased, potentially tied to the strength in data centre activity.
Services are weaker though, with information (-10k), ﬁnancial (-7k),
professional business services (-9k) and government (-17k) all
falling. The chart below shows that the 'big 3' job-creating sectors

remain private education and healthcare services, government
(state & local rather than federal) and leisure and hospitality.
Cumulative employment change since
December 2022 (000s)

### Page 3

Source: Macrobond, ING
Labour market slack points to weak wage
growth
The rise in the unemployment rate shouldn’t be much of a surprise.
It is a low hire, low ﬁre economy and the lack of opportunities has
led to a hefty fall in labour participation. It can be a little choppy,
and we saw a 485,000 increase in the labour force last month,
lifting the participation rate to 61.8% from 61.6%. There are now
similar numbers of unemployed people and job vacancies. In early

2022, there were two job vacancies for every unemployed
American. Unsurprisingly, this demand-supply swing lower is
depressing wage growth, with the low quits rate suggesting further
downside risks to wage growth relative to the current, already low,
3% rate. This suggests there is no inﬂation threat coming from the
jobs market and reinforces our view that the market pricing of 3–4
Fed rate hikes looks too aggressive.
Low quits rate points to ongoing wage

weakness

Document 259: FX Daily_ Fed minutes shouldn’t rock the boat

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Fed minutes shouldn’t rock the boat.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

A hawkish dot plot in September suggests today's FOMC
minutes shouldn’t surprise on the dovish side. At the same
time, softish post-meeting data probably raise the bar for a
positive dollar reaction today. Eyes should remain on oil, which
is back on the rise this morning, and France, which is enjoying
some relief in sovereign spreads

Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Chris Turner
The dollar may stabilise around current levels, but risks remain skewed to the
upside
USD: Looking for clarity in the minutes
A rare good day for bonds let US stocks rise to new highs and left
the safe-haven dollar weaker yesterday. Brent brieﬂy fell below
$100/bbl before recovering after reports that Iran had stepped up
strikes on tankers in the Strait of Hormuz. Stubbornly high oil prices
continue to reﬂect concerns about further disruptions, even as
actual oil ﬂows have improved. With few signs of an imminent deal,
energy prices should remain a drag on any meaningful recovery in
bonds and, by extension, on a decline in the dollar.

### Page 2

Attention turns back to the Fed today with the release of the
September FOMC minutes. Markets are still looking for greater
clarity on the data-policy reaction function, particularly which
inﬂation outcomes would justify another hike this year. The
minutes should also oﬀer some insight into any dovish dissent.
However, with the dot plot showing more members expecting two
further hikes this year (4) than no further tightening (2), the scope
for a dovish surprise appears limited. At the same time, data has

been softish since the September hike and markets continue to
ﬁrmly price a December move, setting a relatively high bar for a
positive USD reaction.
We expect some stabilisation around the 102.0 area in DXY, but
risks remain on the upside.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: Don't count on a French sentiment
rebound
French bonds' premium shrank further yesterday, with the 10y
spread to bunds tightening to 125bp. The move appeared to be
driven primarily by an improvement in global bond market

sentiment and, to a lesser extent, by Marine Le Pen's pledge to
reduce the budget deﬁcit sharply to 3.7% of GDP next year. One
indication of a lingering French premium is the French-Italian 10y
spread, which remains wide at 22bp and has only corrected 5bp
from last week’s peak.
While it’s clear that Le Pen is attempting to establish herself as the
market-friendly candidate, our macro team notes that her plan
currently rests on ambitious spending-cut targets rather than a

fully costed programme, with major uncertainties around how
€140bn of savings, particularly on pensions, would be achieved. We
therefore aren’t convinced her words are enough to drive a material
OAT recovery from here.
The euro welcomed tighter spreads, but the rebound has lost steam
overnight on the back of higher oil prices. A return towards
1.1150/1.1180 remains the risk today. EUR/CHF has now recovered

SEK: Softer inﬂation shouldn't derail
November hike plan
Swedish inﬂation for September surprised slightly on the downside
this morning. Headline CPIF rose as expected to 1.5%, led by energy
prices, but the core measure excluding energy stayed at 0.5%
against expectations of 0.7%.

SEK’s reaction has been muted, which is understandable
considering the Riksbank has been taking low inﬂation with a pinch
of salt given temporary dampening factors, and above all given the
hawkish guidance in September.
We still expect a hike in November (now 18bp priced in), with a
follow-up move in February. EUR/SEK has corrected following some
idiosyncratic EUR weakness, but the external environment (oil,
bonds) remains challenging for SEK, and the prospect of Riksbank

hikes isn’t enough to lift the currency by itself. We still expect a
decline into year-end in EUR/SEK, but our 11.05 target is looking
increasingly hard to reach, with 11.10-11.20 now more realistic.
Francesco Pesole
CEE: September inﬂation rebound
Following the inﬂation spikes seen in Poland last week and the
Czech Republic yesterday, Hungary’s ﬁgures were released this
morning. We saw a rise from this year's low of 1.3% to 1.6%. As in
the other countries, the main driver was higher fuel prices, although

the increase came in below both market and central bank
expectations. In its September forecast, the central bank had
projected inﬂation to rise to 1.7%.
Later today, the National Bank of Poland will decide on monetary
policy; we expect it to leave rates unchanged at 3.75%. Although

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                            October 1, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 19.4% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.9%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 10.6% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 4.3%.

 One companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 100.0% reported 
results exceeding analyst estimates.

 One companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 100.0% reported 
revenue exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of October 5, zero companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through October 1, one companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 100% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 18.1% above estimates, which is above the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

In aggregate, companies are reporting revenues that are 0.4% above estimates, which is below the 1.3% long-term

Q3 2026: EARNINGS GROWTH RATES

Exhibit 6A.  STOXX 600: Q3 2026 Earnings Growth (Based on 303 constituents with data in both current 
and year-ago period) 
 Earnings €B Earnings €B Growth €B Growth % 
Sector 26Q3 25Q3 26Q3 26Q3 
Basic Materials 5.5 4.7 0.8 16.8% 
Consumer Cyclicals 9.1 7.3 1.8 25.0% 
Consumer Non-Cyclicals 3.1 2.9 0.2 8.3% 
Energy 27.9 14.0 13.8 98.6% 
Financials 55.5 50.7 4.8 9.5% 
Healthcare 18.5 17.7 0.9 5.0% 
Industrials 16.3 13.9 2.5 17.7% 
Technology 13.9 11.3 2.7 23.8% 
Real Estate 0.9 3.1 -2.2 -71.4% 
Utilities 5.9 5.7 0.2 3.0% 
STOXX 600 156.8 131.3 25.5 19.4% 
 Source: LSEG I/B/E/S

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 19.4%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €156.8B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.3B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Nine of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improv ement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (98. 6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €27.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-71.4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

 Revenue €B Revenue €B Growth €B Growth %

Real Estate 1.8 2.1 -0.3 -15.4% 
Utilities 83.9 67.7 16.2 23.9% 
STOXX 600 1495.4 1351.7 143.8 10.6% 
 Source: LSEG I/B/E/S

The estimated revenue growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 10.6%. The STOXX 600 is expected to earn 
revenues of €1495.4B in Q3 2026, compared to €1351.7B in Q3 2025.

Six of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in revenues relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest revenue growth rate for the quarter, while the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest revenue growth rate (43.8%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€310.9B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €216.2B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-15.4%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€1.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €2.1B in Q3 2025.

Analysts expect positive earnings growth from 15 of th e 16 countries represented in the STOXX 600 index. Poland 
(91.2%) and Norway (75.2%) have the highest estimated ear nings growth rates, while Ireland (-7.4%) and Germany 
(0.9%) have the lowest estimated growth.

AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
Note: The estimate revision numbers below are an aggregate of the total number of earnings estimate 
revisions for the Fiscal Year 1 period for all companies in the STOXX 600 over the previous seven days. 
Up revisions represent the total number of estimates for Fiscal Year 1 submitted in the past seven days 
that are higher than the previous estimates for Fiscal Year 1. Down revisions represent the total number 
of estimates for Fiscal Year 1 submitted in the past seven days of that are lower than the previous 
estimates for Fiscal Year 1.

Q3 2026 – Q3 2027: EARNINGS GROWTH

The estimated earnings growth rates for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 through Q3 2027 are 19.4%, 35.1%, 14.4%, -
6.2% and 8.1%, respectively. For sector-level growth rates, see Exhibits 2C-6C in this report.

EARNINGS CALENDAR

There are zero STOXX 600 companies with quarterly earnings estimates scheduled to report quarterly earnings during 
the week of October 5, 2026.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 1, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings. 
SECTION A: EARNINGS OUTLOOK

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

13 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 84.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 7.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 5.3% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

63% of companies beat estimates and 37% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 77% of companies beat the 
estimates and 23% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting revenues that are 2.4% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
2002) average surprise factor of 1.3% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 2.5%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q4 is 28.5%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 25.5%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $804.2B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.7B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Real Estate sector 
has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (102.8%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $49.2B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.3B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (230.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (129.3%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39.4%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (54.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $289.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.6B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (117%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.8%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 18.4%. 
The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (4.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $15.2B in 
26Q4, compared to earnings of $14.6B in 25Q4. 2 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Telecom Tower Reits (-13.3%) and Multi-Family Residential Reits (-0.1%) sub-industries 
have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 7.1%.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 11.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth rate declines  to 11.2%. The S&P 
500 expects to see revenue of $4826.7B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of $4324.7B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 
constituents) in 25Q3.

11 of the 11 sectors anticipate revenue growth for the quarter. The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the 
quarter, while the Health Care sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3.

The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate (39.5%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $764.6B in 26Q3, compared to 
revenue of $547.9B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher revenue than a year ago. The Semiconductors 
(87.7%) and Semiconductor Materials & Equipment (48.2%) sub-industries have the highest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are 
removed, the growth rate declines to 22.1%.

The Health Care sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $958.4B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of 
$921.2B in 25Q3. 1 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower revenue than a year ago. The Health Care Supplies (-3.1%) 
and Managed Health Care (1.2%) sub-industries have the lowest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed , the growth rate 
improves to 5.1%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

thought were the most important drivers. Inflation, fiscal fears, and stronger-than-
expected US growth were picked as the most important drivers. 30% picked
inflation as the most important driver even though 10yr US Breakevens have hardly

moved in the selloff.

To highlight the inconsistency, US inflation expectations over the next 5 years
have risen only marginally since we last asked in June, back up to 2.9% from 2.8%.

strong narrative that inflation is causing the bond selloff, it doesn’t stack up with
long-term inflation expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.
“... both the monthly
and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

1. Westpac–DataX Card Tracker Index*
Card activity: soft finish to a strong quarter
The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker presents indicators based on the millions of

credit and debit card transactions processed by Westpac every day. The measures
are a timely guide to shifts in spending. See p 9 for a detailed explanation.
This report is produced by Westpac Economics.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the quarterly
national accounts. All estimates are
adjusted for regular seasonal variations.
Quarterly growth momentum is now

tracking around 1.7%, the strongest pace
since the fuel price spike in March, and
pior to that, since the turn of the year.
• Notably, growth ex fuel has slowed
since the start of September, tracking a
slower 1.4% quarterly pace in the latest
week. This reflects both the end of the
temporary halving in fuel excise tax and
the latest resurgence in energy prices
globally.
• Recall that Q2 national accounts showed
nominal spending (excluding housing

costs, which are not covered in our card
tracker indicators) rose 0.9%qtr, similar
to Q1’s 1%qtr gain but with annual
growth slowing to 4.7%yr from 5.5%yr.
Growth in real, inflation-adjusted terms
was more subdued at +0.4%qtr, 1.8%yr
(near flat in per capita terms). The detail
was ‘messy’ with significant disruptions
from the Middle East conflict (see here).
• The card tracker data now has 12 of the
13 weeks of Q3. As such, the 1.7%qtr
rise in nominal terms and price rise in the

1%qtr range points to a real, inflation
adjusted gain of around 0.7%qtr, in line
with Westpac’s current forecasts.
Card activity points to ~0.7%qtr gain in Q3 in real, inflation adjusted terms

### Page 4

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
4
• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in card
activity across broad categories. The year
has been marked by rotations between
essentials and discretionary categories
as fuel price shocks have washed in
and out. The switch from essentials to
discretionary categories – services in

particular – in the early part of Q3 is again
reversing with the main drag coming in
discretionary goods.
• In quarterly growth terms, essential
goods is now leading, running at 2.5%qtr,
with discretionary services still relatively
strong at 2.2%qtr, essential services
tracking more slowly at 1.4%qtr and
discretionary goods much weaker at just
0.3%qtr (flat to down in per capita terms).
• Digging into the more detailed
discretionary services segments, the

initial quarterly pickup came from a big
turnaround in transport services, which
look to be partly a rebound from air travel
disruptions in Q2 with a similar pattern
evident in ‘international transactions’
(which are not included in discretionary
services category, as they include some
goods purchases, but are presented here
as corroborating evidence). The charts
on p6-7 provide some more detail on the
other categories. Notably the weakening
in discretionary goods momentum looks

to be broad-based.
• Regionally, the softening is a little more
evident in Vic than in other states.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 5
• Our MHSI and retail card indexes 
are composite measures based on 
transactions in categories that are in 
scope for the ABS monthly household 
spending indicator (MHSI) and retail 
sales estimates (which have been based 
on the MHSI detail since the ABS retail 
survey was discontinued in June 2025). 
• As noted previously, the retail and 
MHSI index measures significantly 
outperformed the broader WCTI in 
Q2 but with the gap narrowing in July-
August. The latest weekly signals now 
show both more in line at 1.6%qtr and 
1.7%qtr respectively. 
• The retail card index measure is a 
narrower subset of spend that excludes 
fuel and most services except hospitality. 
Chart 6 shows a sharp weakening in 
non food retail in recent weeks with 
food retail also stalling but hospitality 
tracking a firmer, albeit slowing pace. 
• The ABS MHSI was a little firmer than 
we expected but came in below market 
expectations in August with a flat result, 
the 3mth growth rate ticking up to 
2.2%qtr. Note that a significant part of the 
quarterly strength is coming from vehicle 
sales which surged through the middle of 
the year (see here for more).

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/21
本行長期追蹤美國銀行信用卡消費統計數據(Consumer
checkpoimt，月報) 顯示，8月消費動能自7月疲弱中明顯
反彈。從美國銀行發行的信用卡、簽帳卡合計消費YoY成
長率已連續三個月降溫（6月6.3%→7月5.0%→8月4.5%）
剔除油品後同步走緩（5.6%→4.3%→3.7%），主因6月世
界盃與線上促銷時序錯位帶來的高基期效應消退。惟季調
後MoM動能出現反轉，7月一度下滑（-0.2%），8月明顯
回升（+0.9%）。商務部數據同步佐證：8月零售銷售季調
後月增1.2%，優於市場預期0.7%，成長動能來自線上零售、
加油站（價格推升）及餐飲、電子產品等類別。

《「K型」分化持續收斂，低所得消費並非來自舉債》
8 月非必需消費 YoY成長 ， 高所得 （ 5.9% ） 與低所得
（5.7%）差距僅0.2個百分點 ，為2024年1月以來最小；
中所得 （5.1%）略為落後 。低所得族群稅後工資成長已
連續第二個月領先其他所得層。
另紐約聯準銀行資料顯示 ，2026年第二季家庭總負債為

18.771兆美元 ，季減130億美元 ，年增3,830億美元；信
用卡餘額季增210億美元、年增540億美元至1.263兆美元，
負債溫和成長，並非急遽加槓桿。嚴重違約（90天以上）
的 年 化 轉 出 率 方 面， 信 用 卡 由 去 年 同 期6.93% 微升至
6.97%。這與美銀 Checkpoint 揭露的另一項正面訊號互
相呼應：所有所得層級「每月全額繳清信用卡帳單」的家
庭比例都在上升，僅繳最低應繳金額的比例雖也上升但幅
度較小。綜合來看，原先市場擔憂的低所得者會藉由舉債
消費，但目前數據並不支持這樣的看法 ，等於是提供K型
收斂假說的有力證據

大學消費者信心指數9月初值急跌至47.8，創自1978年以

來次低。

《4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up需求強弱度分析》

由於NRF尚未發布2026年假期旺季（11-12月）專屬銷售
預測（歷年慣例於10-11月發布），且目前可用最新官方
預測為發生在中東戰爭之前。因此改採bottom-up方式，
以供需鏈前置指標、零售商財測、消費者信心與成本端壓
力四大面向交叉驗證。
➢ NRF Global Port Tracker預估9月將為全年主要貨櫃港
口進口量最高的月份，異於往年7-8月峰期的季節性規
律，顯示零售商對4Q旺季需求仍持樂觀，提前備貨以
應對可能的關稅／運輸不確定性。
➢ 零售商財測訊號分化，Target（同比銷售+3.8%，營收
+5.3%，全年EPS指引上調至$9.90-10.90）與Best
Buy（同比銷售+4.1%，全年指引上調至營收$42.3-
42.8bn，同店銷售1.9-3%）均上調全年指引。

4
，反映電子／電腦、家居、美妝、新品類需求扎實。相較
之下，Walmart雖也上調全年指引 ，但Q3單季指引轉軟
（EPS $0.62-0.64，同店銷售3.0-3.75%），股價在公布
後重挫9%——市場解讀為Q2超預期主要來自一次性關稅
退款效應（約750個基點的毛利貢獻 ），而非需求結構性
改善 ，該退款已大幅讓利投入價格競爭 （單季推出超過

1.1萬項降價），意味著Q2的優異表現部分係提前透支Q3。
同時電商與數位通路持續為成長引擎： Walmart全球電商
+23% 、 Target數位同店 +8.7% 、 Home Depot 線上
+11%（連續五季雙位數成長 ）、Lowe's線上+15.7%，
顯示全通路投資持續帶動業績。
➢ 消費者「精打細算」特徵明顯：off-price（折扣）業態
如TJX、Ross Stores同店成長強勁（分別+4%、
+10%），反映消費者尋求高性價比商品的行為持續強
化。
➢ 關稅退款效應將於Q3起逐漸消退，市場焦點將轉向排
除一次性利益後的核心毛利率與同店銷售動能是否可持
續。
➢ 燃油與運費成本為共同逆風：各公司財報及法說會均提
及油價、運輸成本上升對下半年毛利率的壓力。
結論：4Q26傳統旺季需求呈現「量能可望維持、獲利
品質面臨分化壓力」的中性格局，與兩個月前「消費持續
熱絡」的單一樂觀敘事相比，風險報酬比已明顯向下傾斜
(亦即產業獲利成長動能趨緩)，整體消費的評價面難有向
上提升的動力來源。

### Page 1

专题研究报告
中小企业发展系列研究（2）：德国金融支持隐形冠军企业发
展的经验
核心观点 发展研究·专题研究
证券分析师：薛冰
0755-81982126
xuebing1@guosen.com.cn
S0980524060002
相关研究报告
《中小企业发展系列研究（1）：德国隐形冠军企业的发展经验》
——2026-09-10
德国以“大实体、小金融”的发展理念践行“金融服务实体”，注重通过银
行信贷、政策性金融及多层次资本市场促进中小企业的发展，形成了数量庞
大的中小企业群体，并孕育出一批在细分市场占据全球领先地位的隐形冠军
企业，在金融支持隐形冠军企业发展方面积累的众多有益经验。
德国银行体系是金融支持隐形冠军企业的主力军，注重各类银行错位发展、
关系型融资与区域深耕。一是银行体系错位支持。德国银行形成了私人商业
银行、储蓄银行与信用社三大支柱，私人商业银行聚焦大型跨国企业与高端
客户，储蓄银行坚守属地经营服务本地中小企业，信用社依托熟人网络覆盖
小微企业，实现对各规模隐形冠军企业的对接。二是管家银行构建关系型融

资。管家银行与企业形成长期合作机制，有效破解信息不对称，为隐形冠军
企业技术升级与长远发展提供中长期贷款支持。三是区域银行体系深耕地
方。储蓄银行、信用社等凭借其庞大的分支机构深度服务所在区域的企业，
使银行与区域产业结成长期利益共同体。
德国以政策性金融为隐形冠军企业发展保驾护航。一是政策性银行复兴信贷
银行（KfW）提供转贷服务。KfW通过合作伙伴银行以优惠利率向企业放款，
并按产品类型分担信用风险；转贷产品的期限与利率设计匹配隐形冠军“长
周期、重研发”的资金需求特征。二是担保银行提供担保，政府提供再担保。
担保银行为无法提供硬抵押品的企业提供信用增信；以“政府—担保银行—
商业银行”三层风险分担结构分散损失压力，破解中小企业“缺抵押”难题、
降低中小企业融资门槛。
德国以多层次资本市场拓宽隐形冠军企业的成长通道。一是金字塔式股权市
场。德国形成了由受监管市场和公开市场组成的分层体系，其中公开市场中
包含面向中小企业的板块（Scale），为快速成长的中小企业提供股权和债
权融资。二是债券市场提供支持。债务凭证（Schuldschein）等债券工具为

中型企业提供低成本、较长期限的资金；中小企业公开债券补充适配部分高
风险企业。三是股权投资与引导基金加快企业培育。私募股权、风险投资适
配高研发、高成长的中小企业；政策性引导基金的股权投资加快隐形冠军企
业培育。
风险提示：政策调整不及预期；海外市场风险波动。

### Page 4

1.引言
德国长期坚持“大实体、小金融”的发展理念。所谓“大实体”，是指实体经济
在德国经济体系中占据绝对主导地位，制造业、精密装备、汽车、化工等高端制
造部门始终是产业根基，德国也因此被长期视为欧洲的“工业心脏”；而“小金
融”则指金融部门保持适当的规模，重点服务于实体经济。这一理念的实质是，
金融市场的发展规模和节奏以产业融资的客观需求为边界，金融与实体经济之间
形成“金融依附于产业、资本服务制造”的良性关系。近些年，德国制造业增加
值表现出上升的趋势，而金融业增加值走势较为平稳，从占 GDP的比重可以看出，
德国金融业的增加值占比长期保持在 5%以下，而制造业占比位于 20%左右。
图1：德国金融业与制造业增加值（十亿欧元）
资料来源：Wind，德国统计局，国信证券经济研究所整理
图2：德国金融业与制造业增加值占 GDP比重（%）
资料来源：Wind，世界银行，国信证券经济研究所整理

### Page 5

在德国金融服务实体经济的过程中，对中小企业的扶持尤为重要。中小企业是德
国经济的重要组成部分，既构成就业与产出的基础，也是大批隐形冠军企业孕育
生长的土壤。因此，德国金融体系从制度设计之初就把服务中小企业作为重要命
题，并贯穿于银行、政策性金融与资本市场各环节。遍布城乡的储蓄银行和信用
合作社依托属地经营与关系型融资，贴近本地中小企业的真实经营状况，提供稳
定、长期、低波动的信贷支持；以复兴信贷银行为代表的政策性金融机构，则在
市场失灵处发挥引导和托底作用，通过转贷、贴息、担保等方式降低中小企业的
融资门槛与融资成本，尤其在逆周期和经济转型时期成为中小企业最可靠的资金
后盾。对于中小企业的贷款是德国商业贷款的主要组成，根据经济合作与发展组
织（OECD）2026 年的研究，2024 年德国中小企业未偿还的商业贷款金额为 6520
亿欧元，占未偿还的商业贷款的比例为 67.68%。
图3：德国中小企业商业贷款金额（十亿欧元）及占比（%）
资料来源：OECD，国信证券经济研究所整理
图4：德国中小企业商业新增贷款金额（十亿欧元）及占比（%）

资料来源：OECD，国信证券经济研究所整理

### Page 6

正是德国金融对中小企业的支持，才孕育出了众多隐形冠军企业。德国经济在发
展过程中，形成了数量庞大的中小企业，其竞争优势也正是来自这些富有活力、
勇于创新的中小企业。虽然德国的世界 500强企业位于中国、美国、日本、法国
之后，但是在专业细分领域德国具有明显优势，隐形冠军企业数量世界领先。西
蒙统计了 2016年全球的 2734家“隐形冠军”，其中德国有 1307 家，以机械工程
和机器制造类居多（占比 37%），属于在专业细分市场占有领先地位、但公众知
名度不太高的中小型企业。这些企业不急于扩大规模或进行广告营销，而是强调
员工技能培训、广泛听取各方意见，用过硬品质赢得用户口碑。
图5：2025年世界 500强企业分布（%）
资料来源：《财富》杂志，国信证券经济研究所整理
图6：全球隐形冠军数量（家）
资料来源：《隐形冠军：未来全球化的先锋》赫尔曼·西蒙，国信证券经济研究所整理

### Page 7

2.银行体系支持隐形冠军企业发展
2.1 银行体系错位支持隐形冠军企业发展
隐形冠军企业在经营规模、技术复杂度、客户结构和国际化程度方面差异显著，
需要多元化的银行体系提供配套支持。德国隐形冠军企业的规模差异较大，低至
数千万欧元的营业额，高至十亿欧元以上的营业额。意味着企业融资需求的规模、
期限、担保方式和信息处理模式都各不相同，大型跨国客户集中度高的隐形冠军
需要中长期项目融资和跨境结算服务，身处本地供应链的隐形冠军需要小额、灵
活的流动资金贷款，而扎根于小城镇的微型供应商则依赖日常结算和风险共担的
信用支持。正是这种需求的异质性，构成了德国不同类型的银行在规模、客户定
位、风险偏好和治理结构上形成互补，为处于不同成长阶段、不同产业链位置的
隐形冠军企业提供金融服务。
图7：德国隐形冠军企业营业额分布
资料来源：《Deutschlands versteckte Stars: Die Hidden Champions》，国信证券经济研究所整理
德国银行体系按照所有制和经营目标划分为三大支柱——私人商业银行

（Commercial Banks）、储蓄银行（Savings Banks）与信用社（Credit
Cooperatives），三者在法律形式、经营目标与客户定位上形成系统性的错位分
工。私人商业银行采用股份公司等私法形式，以股东利润最大化为目标；储蓄银
行属于公法机构，受公法管辖，以履行区域公共使命为首要目标；信用社采用注
册合作社形式，以社员利益和服务促进为核心。根据德意志联邦银行（Bundesbank）
的统计，2025年末德国共有 1266家信贷机构，总资产规模达 9.48万亿欧元。三
大支柱在机构数量上仍占绝对主导：私人商业银行 232 家（含 3 家大银行、129
家区域性商业银行和 100家外资银行分行），储蓄银行体系 342家，信用合作社
646家。按机构数计算，私人商业银行约占 18.3%，储蓄银行体系约占 27.0%，信
用合作社约占 51.0%。不同的银行类型决定了不同的服务对象与风险承担边界，
从而在经济分工中自然形成“大客户服务大城市、中型客户服务区域、微型客户

服务社区”的层级体系，使各类隐形冠军企业能找到与之匹配的融资渠道。

### Page 8

图8：德国各类型银行数量
资料来源：德意志联邦银行，国信证券经济研究所整理
私人商业银行凭借综合金融服务能力与全球网络，主要聚焦服务大型跨国企业与
高端客户群体。德意志银行（Deutsche Bank）、德国商业银行（Commerzbank）
等大型银行持有全牌照，能够提供贷款、资本市场承销、贸易融资、外汇交易和
并购顾问等一站式服务。在支持隐形冠军方面，大型商业银行的作用更多体现在
企业规模扩张的阶段，当隐形冠军成长为中型跨国公司，需要大额中长期项目融
资、跨境并购资金或上市承销服务时，私人商业银行的全球网络和产品线优势便
体现出来。
储蓄银行坚守属地经营原则，将区域发展与公共福利置于盈利目标之上，是服务
本地中小企业的中坚力量。储蓄银行不能跨区域经营，只能在设立时划定的服务
区域内吸收存款和发放贷款，并购也仅限于相邻区域，通过“区域经营原则”
（Regionalprinzip）确保金融服务覆盖“每一个角落”。这一原则从制度层面保
障了储蓄银行的资金不会从欠发达地区流向发达城市，而是留在本地循环。储蓄

银行通过与企业建立长期关系，将企业经营数据、业主品格和行业认知转化为信
用判断依据，是应对隐形冠军企业信息不透明特征的有效机制。
信用社依托熟人网络和成员互助机制，精准对接小微企业最基层的融资需求。信
用社的规模普遍较小，其决策流程更灵活，对单笔小额贷款的处理成本相对更低，
因而能够覆盖传统大型银行和储蓄银行都难以触达的最小微型企业和个体经营
者，为隐形冠军企业在研发密集期和市场培育期提供了低成本、长周期的资金支
持。
2.2 管家银行构建关系型融资
管家银行（Hausbank，又称为主办银行或主银行，即 PrimaryBank）是德国企业
与银行之间形成的一种长期合作机制。中小企业融资难的核心症结在于重要信息
缺失——缺乏经审计的财务数据、可抵押资产与信用评级，导致银行无法按标准
化的交易型信贷模型进行风险定价。管家银行通过包揽企业的结算、信贷、投资
银行咨询等核心金融业务，在长期互动中持续积累企业主的信誉、管理能力、市
场地位与真实现金流等信息，将不可传递的私人信息转化为可用的风控依据。从

### Page 9

合约经济学视角看，这一机制是银行以“关系专用性投资”换取信息租金、企业
以“锁定”换取长期稳定信贷可得性的跨期互惠安排。银行在前期承担了信息生
产成本，因而在后续获得定价与交叉销售的收益；企业则获得了在经营困难时不
致被“抽贷”的隐性承诺。正是通过这种长期、稳定的银企绑定，德国将一次性
信贷交易转化为跨周期的信息生产过程，有效破解了中小企业融资中的信息不对
称难题。根据德国复兴信贷银行（KfW）2019年的一项研究，有 93%的德国中小企
业拥有自己的管家银行（主办银行），中小企业信贷量的 80%来自管家银行，截
至 2017 年，企业与管家银行平均合作时长为 20.2年。
管家银行关系的直接制度效果体现在信贷期限结构上——中长期贷款占据绝对
主导，为隐形冠军的技术升级与设备投资提供了与回报周期相匹配的资金预期。
银行信贷期限与实体投资回报周期的错配，是中小企业陷入“短贷长用”、被迫
频繁展期与滚动续贷的根源，也使其极易在信贷环境收紧时遭遇流动性危机。管
家银行因掌握企业跨周期的真实经营信息，敢于提供与设备折旧周期、技术研发

周期相匹配的长期资金，而无需依赖短期续贷来控制风险。这类长期资金在功能
上等价于“耐心资本”——它使隐形冠军能够在不稀释控制权、不公开财务信息、
不承受资本市场短期业绩压力的前提下完成长周期投入，恰好契合家族企业“跨
代传承优先于规模扩张”的目标函数。根据经济合作与发展组织（OECD）2026 年
的一项研究，德国中小企业的长期贷款远远高于短期贷款，2024 年德国中小企业
的长期贷款金额为 5670亿欧元，而同期短期贷款金额仅为 170 亿欧元。
图9：德国中小企业短期贷款与长期贷款金额（十亿欧元）
资料来源：OECD，国信证券经济研究所整理
2.3 区域银行体系注重服务隐形冠军企业
隐形冠军企业在德国各州分布较为广泛，其中密度最高的是汉堡。根据德国企业
Listflix 对德国 2935 家隐形冠军企业统计，隐形冠军企业在德国全国分布的平
均水平是每 10 万人有 3.51家。并且，汉堡的密度最高，每 10万人中有 7.36 家
隐形冠军企业，高出全国平均水平 110%。隐形冠军企业密度较高的州还有不来梅、

黑森、巴登-符腾堡等，每 10 万人中的隐形冠军企业分别为 4.97 家、4.89 家、
4.74家。

### Page 10

图10：每 10万人中隐形冠军企业在德国各州的分布
资料来源：Listflix，国信证券经济研究所整理
隐形冠军在区域发展中扮演着关键角色。在德国及德语国家（或地区），其中一
些传统行业的隐形冠军企业集群拥有数百年历史根基，例如索林根（Solingen）
的刀具集群、施韦因富特（Schweinfurt）的滚子轴承集群、贝吉施兰（Bergisches
Land）地区的锁具技术集群，以及纽伦堡地区的铅笔集群。一些传统产业集群延
续到了 21世纪，例如索林根的“刀-剪刀-刀片”产业集群，其他传统产业集群则
已消失，如皮尔马森斯（Pirmasens）周边的鞋业集群或伍珀塔尔（Wuppertal）
的纺织业集群。此外，一些起步较晚的产业集群也充满了大量的隐形冠军企业，
这些集群包括霍恩洛厄（Hohenlohe）的风机集群、西威斯特法伦（eastern
Westphalia）的接口技术集群、德国西南部的微电子集群、德国北部的风能集群，
以及柏林的电子商务集群（Simon，2022）。

### Page 12

隐形冠军企业的区域分布，需要金融机构能够扎根区域、长期陪伴企业成长。储
蓄银行与信用社遵循的区域经营原则（Regionalprinzip），从法律层面确保本地
吸收的存款主要用于本地放贷。这一原则把银行的资产负债两端锁定在同一区域
经济体内，使银行的长期盈利与本地产业兴旺形成利益共同体——银行无法在区
域经济下行时抽身离场，只能在繁荣时积累、在困难时施救。同时，严格的地域
划分有效避免了机构之间的同质化与内卷式恶性竞争，保护了关系型融资，使银
行有激励为中小企业提供跨周期服务而非短期逐利。根据德意志联邦银行统计，
侧重于服务所在区域企业发展的储蓄银行和信用社的分支机构数量是所有类型银
行中最多的。
图11：德国各类型银行分支机构数量
资料来源：德意志联邦银行，国信证券经济研究所整理
3.政策性金融为隐形冠军企业提供保障
3.1 复兴信贷银行（KfW）提供转贷服务
德国复兴信贷银行（KfW）是德国政策性金融体系的核心主体，其“补充性”与
“中立性”的双重作用可以使其在不挤出商业金融的前提下填补中小企业融资的

市场空白。德国通过法律框架为 KfW 设定了明确的边界：补充性原则要求其以中
小企业融资占比 40%为发展目标；中立性原则要求其避免与商业性银行直接竞争
或导致银行间的不公平竞争。这套制度设计使政策性金融与商业金融形成互补而
非替代关系。从规模看，截至 2025年年底，德国政策性银行资产规模约 1.52 万
亿欧元，占银行业总资产约 13.9%，已略高于信用社的 11.3%，堪称德国银行业的
“第四支柱”。2025年 KfW核心的国内促进业务（贷款与赠款）达 610亿欧元，
较 2024 年的 458 亿欧元增长三分之一；其中面向中小企业与初创企业的资金达
235亿欧元（2024年为 134亿欧元），增幅超过 75%。

### Page 13

图12：德国中小企业政策性资助贷款流程示意图
资料来源：《政府主导下的融资机制与中小企业融资困境——基于德国银行体系视角》，国信证券经济研
究所整理
转贷模式是 KfW 运行中的关键机制——KfW 不向最终客户直接放贷，而是将资金
提供给合作伙伴银行，由后者以优惠利率贷给企业，从而在制度上把政策性银行
与商业银行从竞争对手转为利益共同体。在这一模式下，企业不能直接向 KfW 申
请政策性贷款，而必须经由其融资伙伴（商业银行、储蓄银行或信用社）提交申
请；融资伙伴先对企业的财务状况、投资方案与商业计划进行审查，完成筛选后
才将申请转交 KfW；KfW批准并出具资金承诺书后，由融资伙伴与企业签订贷款协
议并负责放款。这一流程设计有三重功效：一是解决了 KfW 不设分支网络、缺乏
客户触达能力的短板；二是把贷前审查与贷后管理保留在最了解企业的银行手中；
三是使商业银行通过利差获得合理收益，从而有动力持续参与。数据显示，2019
年 KfW 向银行发放的贷款中转贷贷款占比达 70%以上。从成效看，根据崔颖等

（2025）的研究，KfW 通过与商业银行构建的转贷机制，可以为中小企业平均节
省融资成本 20%—30%。
转贷模式的激励相容性，集中体现在KfW与合作银行之间的信用风险分担安排上。
KfW 按产品类型承担不同的信用风险，银行始终保留剩余风险，从而避免了“银
行只赚利差、不承担风险”的道德风险。从合约设计的机理看，若政策性资金完
全兜底风险，合作银行将丧失审慎筛选借款人的激励，可能把本不符合条件的项
目推送给政策性银行，最终形成公共资金的漏损。德国的做法是按产品风险特征
设定差异化的责任免除比例，对于投资风险较高的初创公司，KfW 的风险分担比

### Page 14

例可以达到 80%，而对于中型企业的政策性贷款，KfW 的风险分担比例可以降至
50%。这一梯度安排使风险越高、政策性兜底越厚，同时银行始终有“风险自留”
的硬约束。

资料来源：stripe，国信证券经济研究所整理
转贷产品通过期限与利率设计，精准匹配隐形冠军“长周期、重研发”的资金需
求特征。隐形冠军的技术升级与设备投资回报周期往往长达十年以上。KfW 凭借
主权信用获得的长期低成本资金，使其有能力提供与实体投资周期相匹配的长期

限产品，并通过“本息优惠机制”进一步降低企业前期压力——资助贷款设有 1
年至 10 年不等的免本金偿还期，期内企业只需支付利息而无需偿还本金。从
KfW2025年对中小企业贷款的投放结构看，创新与数字化融资新承诺额达 27亿欧
元，较 2024 年的 15 亿欧元近乎翻倍，反映政策性资金正加大对技术升级的定向
支持；气候与环境领域促进业务量升至 138 亿欧元（2024 年为 54 亿欧元），其
中“可再生能源—标准”计划承诺 92 亿欧元。就覆盖面而言，2025 年 KfW 新业
务触及 13.7 万家企业，相当于德国每年获得贷款的中小企业中约 10%；同时支持
3.5万名创业者，约占依赖外部融资的德国初创企业的 10%。此外，KfW 通过“商
政分离”的职能重组，由中小企业银行专司低息贷款与信用担保等政策工具，由
KfW IPEX 进出口项目融资银行面向海外拓展企业提供市场化融资，实现企业从初
创到国际化的资金全覆盖。

### Page 15

3.2 担保银行与政府提供再担保
担保银行是德国专为破解中小企业“缺抵押”难题而设立的准公共机构，其制度
价值在于把企业无法提供的硬抵押品，转换为担保机构的信用增信，从而打通银
行信贷的最后一公里。从信贷合约的机理看，银行贷款决策的核心约束是违约损
失率，而抵押品的作用正是在违约发生时为银行提供第二还款来源。隐形冠军与
科技型中小企业多为轻资产结构，核心资产是专利、技术诀窍与人力资本，这些
资产缺乏活跃的二手市场、变现能力弱，银行难以接受其作为合格抵押品。担保
银行的功能正是以自身信用替代缺失的抵押品，把不可抵押的资产转化为可授信
的债权。德国目前共有 17家担保银行专门为缺乏抵押物的中小企业、特别是科技
型初创企业提供贷款担保，年担保贷款总额超过 20亿欧元，占中小企业融资总量
的 2%左右。根据德国担保银行协会（VDB）的统计，2025 年德国有 5072个项目获
得了担保银行的担保，比 2024 年增长了 13.5%。
担保体系的效力来自“政府—担保银行—商业银行”的三层风险分担结构，其中

的每一层都保留风险敞口，既分散了单一机构的损失压力，又避免了任何一方完
全免责导致的道德风险。从风险分配的机理看，若担保全额兜底，商业银行将丧
失审慎动机；若担保比例过低，则不足以改变银行的授信决策。所以，德国在担
保体系的设计中，在单笔贷款层面，担保贷款违约风险通常由担保银行与商业银
行按 80%和 20%的比例共同分担，即商业银行必须自担至少 20%的风险，且不得享
有优先受偿权或特殊抵押品，这一“风险自留”条款是防止银行放松审查的关键
约束。在此之上，联邦政府与各州政府设立反担保（再担保）机制，对担保银行
的风险损失给予 65%-80%补偿支持，确保担保体系的可持续运转。经此三层分摊，
一笔原本银行不愿承接的轻资产贷款，其风险被稀释到银行、担保银行、联邦与
州财政四个主体身上，任何一方的最大损失都被控制在可承受区间。在金额分层
上，担保需求 200万欧元以下的由担保银行或信用担保协会承做，200万至 2000
万欧元的由各州或州立发展银行负责，2000万欧元以上的适用联邦大额担保计划

（主要面向结构薄弱地区）；单个借款人在担保银行项目下的担保总额上限通常
为 200万至 250万欧元，担保期限最长可达 20年。
担保审批流程与管家银行既有信贷流程的深度嵌合，是德国担保体系能够兼顾审
慎与效率的关键，显著降低了中小企业获得增信的成本。德国担保银行把担保机
制嵌入管家银行的既有流程：担保申请须由企业的管家银行提交，银行先行完成
客户筛选与项目评估，担保银行在此基础上作增信决策，形成“银行前端把关、
担保后端增信”的分工。为提高时效性，部分州还开设快速通道，如北威州担保
银行（BürgschaftsbankNRW）的快速担保产品承诺在 5个工作日内完成审批。在
保障范围上，担保覆盖贷款本金债权以及必要的终止与法律追索费用，但不覆盖
利息、担保佣金与滞纳金等衍生费用，由此明确了担保机构的风险边界。

### Page 16

图13：德国由政府担保的中小企业贷款金额（十亿欧元）
资料来源：OECD，国信证券经济研究所整理
在德国政策性银行贷款、政府担保等政策措施的共同作用下，德国中小企业的贷
款利率保持在合理水平。根据经济合作与发展组织（OECD）的统计，德国中小企
业贷款利率比大型企业贷款利率稍高，2020年德国中小企业贷款利率为 2.1%，高
出大型企业 0.6个百分点；2024 年德国中小企业贷款利率为 5.3%，高出大型企业
0.8 个百分点。同时，部分年份中小企业贷款利率还比大型企业低，2023年中小
企业贷款利率为 5.4%，而大型企业为 5.5%。
图14：德国中小企业及大型企业贷款利率（%）对比
